FIRST VOLUME
IN WHICH ARE INDICATED
THE FEASTS AND FASTS, READINGS AND SERVICES
OF THE HOLY ARMENIAN CHURCH
ESTABLISHED
BY OUR HOLY ENLIGHTENER [SAINT GREGORY]
AT THE BEGINNING OF THE ENLIGHTENMENT
OF THE ARMENIAN NATION,
AND ARRANGED
BY SAINT SAHAK PARTEV
AND BY OUR BLESSED PATRIARCHS,
PATRIARCH GIUT
AND YOVHANNES MANDAKUNI,
AND FINALLY,
BY SAINT NERSES SHNORHALI,
AND IN LATER TIMES,
AT THE HOLY SEE OF ETCHMIADZIN,
BY LORD SIMEON, CATHOLICOS OF THE ARMENIANS.
PRINTING HOUSE OF THE APOSTOLIC SEE
OF SAINTS JAMES
JERUSALEM
[In the Year of the Armenians] 1350 [= 1901]
1901

## page_0457_human

CANON OF THE THEOPHANY
AND BIRTH OF CHRIST OUR GOD

In the night there is a vigil with psalms and services.
And in the morning, the Blessing hymn: Tone 6 [gy] "Great Mystery" (Khorhourd mets). But the Patriarch, the priests, and all the ministers shall vest, and with lighted torches and candles they shall come into the chancel, and thus they shall sing the Blessing hymn. Likewise at the "Lord of heaven" (Ter yerknits) hymns, all being vested, they shall come into the chancel. And at the completion of the "Lord of heaven" hymns, the Patriarch or one of the honorable clergy shall go up to the Altar and they shall say "Proschume" [Orthiu]. Gospel according to Luke 2:8, "And there were shepherds..." to verse 14, "Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men." And they shall say "Glory to God in the highest" up to the middle, and then they shall read the remainder of the Gospel, Luke 2:15, "And it came to pass..." to verse 20, "as it was told unto them." And then they shall sing the rest of the "Glory to God in the highest." Morning Song: "Sun of Justice" (Aregakn ardaroutyan); Response: "In the beauty of holiness" (I vayelchoutyun srbots).

But if it falls on a Sunday, they shall read the Gospel of the Myrrh-bearers. Litany: "Holy Mother" (Mayr sourb). Prayer: "We thank You" (Gohanamk zken). "Holy God, who were born and revealed."
Hymn: Tone 8 [dk] "Great Mystery" (Khorhourd mets). After: "Proschume" [Orthiu]. Gospel according to Luke 1:1, "Forasmuch as many..." to verse 25, "...among men."
Second hymn: "Today they rejoice" (Aysor tsndan). After: "Proschume" [Orthiu]. Gospel according to Luke 1:26, "And in the month..." to verse 38, "...and the angel departed from her."
Third hymn: "Today the Word" (Aysor bann). After: "Proschume" [Orthiu]. Gospel according to Luke 1:39, "And Mary arose..." to verse 80, "...his showing unto Israel."
Fourth hymn: "Dawning from the Father" (I hore tsag). After: "Proschume" [Orthiu]. Gospel according to Luke 2:1, "And it came to pass in those days..." to verse 7, "...room in the inn."
Psalm 109 [110], Antiphon: "In the beauty [of holiness]." Response: "The Lord said unto my Lord." After: "Proschume" [Orthiu].
Gospel according to Matthew 1:1, "The book of the generation of Jesus Christ..." to verse 17, "...fourteen generations."

If it falls on a Sunday, they shall say "Newly Created" (Norasteghtsial), and if on another day, the "Creator-hymn" (Ararchakann) according to the day; and after, let them know [to say] "Hidden Mystery" (Tatskyal khorhourdn). Litany: "Let us ask" (Khndresgouk). "Save [us]" (Ketso). Prayer: "To Your all-powerful and wondrous Birth and Revelation, Christ our God."
Introit of the Liturgy: "The Mother of God, confessing you" (Astvatsatsin zkez khostovanyal). Response: "My soul shall magnify" (Metsatsoustse). Hymn: Tone 3 [ey] "The Holy Mother of God" (Sourb zastvatsatsinn). Psalm 2, Antiphon: "The Lord said unto me." Response: "Why did the heathen rage?"
The Apostle: Titus 2:11, "For there has appeared..." to verse 15, "...Let no man despise thee."
Alleluia: "Today is born He who is from the Father."
Gospel according to Matthew 1:18, "Now the birth of Jesus Christ..." to verse 25, "...His name Jesus."
Hymn of the Hagiology [Sanctus]: "The multitude [of angels]" (Bazmoutyun) is used during these eight days.

## page_0458_auto

And after the Liturgy, they shall go to the water with Cross and Gospel, singing the hymn in Tone 3 [gd]: "Light from Light" (Loys i lue). After this: Psalm 28 [29], antiphon: "The voice of the Lord is upon [the waters]", response: "Ascribe to the Lord". And they shall read the scriptures:
The Prophet Isaiah 12:3, "Thus says the Lord: and you shall draw..." to verse 6, "...in the midst of them."
The Book of Joshua 3:14, "And it came to pass when [the people] set out..." to verse 17, "...passed over on dry ground."
The Book of Exodus 15:22, "And [Moses] led..." to verse 26, "...and he tested them."
The Book of Fourth Kings [2 Kings] 2:19, "And the men of the city said..." to verse 22, "...which he spoke."
The Prophet Ezekiel 47:1, "And he brought me..." to verse 12, "...and their outlet for healing."
The Apostle: 1 Corinthians 10:1, "I do not want [you to be ignorant]..." to verse 4, "...was Christ himself."
Alleluia: "Let the heavens rejoice."
Gospel: Matthew 3:1, "In those days..." to verse 17, "...with whom I am well pleased."
And the deacon proclaims what is in the directory. And they shall read the prayers of Saint Basil; they shall say the great litany aloud, and the other chants; they shall place only one Cross into the water. And the bishop, taking the holy sign [the Cross] and the holy Muron in hand, shall sign the water, saying: "May this water be blessed and sanctified by the sign of this holy Cross, and by this holy Muron, and by this holy Gospel, etc.", repeating this thrice. And then, taking the Cross out of the water, they shall sing the hymn in Tone 4 [dz]: "The Dove Sent Forth" (Arakeloy aghavnoy). And the bishop shall pour the holy Muron into the water.
Litany: "By this holy Cross." Prayer: "Guard us." "Our Father who art in heaven."

And they shall go into the church and perform the Pre-festal service of John the Baptist. Hymn, Tone 1 [ak]: "The Sun" (Aregakann) (and if they have gone outside, they shall sing it as they return). And at the start of the "Fathers" [hymn], everyone shall kiss the holy Gospel. And at the completion of the Pre-festal service: "Christ our God, for the sake of the holy Cross." Litany: "By the holy ascetics." Prayer: "You who chose and received."

During these eight days, the morning song is "Sun of righteousness." Litany: "Holy Mother of God." "Holy God... who wast born and didst reveal thyself." And after the "Holy God", the Psalm of the Gospel: Psalm 109 [110], antiphon: "In the beauty [of holiness]", response: "The Lord said to my Lord." And the Midday introits are three; say one of them each day.
In the Evening: "Bow down [Your ear]" and "Save [me]."
The Gradual Psalms (Mesedi) are three; say one of them each day:
a. "The Lord said to me." b. "He shall come down like rain." c. "He shall magnify." Litany: "Holy Mother of God." For the "Hambartsi" [hymn], say the Blessing hymn of the following day. But after "Compassionate Father", they shall go out together to the chancel with Cross and Gospel, incense and candles, and performing the Pre-festal service of the coming day with its proper "Fathers" hymn, they shall do so during these eight days. Litany: "Holy Mother of God." Prayer: "Receive, Lord, our supplications."

On the Second Day in the morning, Tone 2 [ahg]: "Today the Word from the Father", with its proper service. Alleluia of the Nativity. Psalm 109 [110], antiphon: "In the beauty [of holiness]", response: "The Lord said to my Lord." Gospel: Mark 1:1, "The beginning of the gospel..." to verse 13, "...and [the angels] ministered to him." During these eight days, say the "Ararchakan" [hymn] according to the proper day. Litany: "Let us ask." Prayer: "To You, O Lord." Midday Introit: "We beseech you." Psalm 5 [6], antiphon: "Lord, as with a shield", response: "Give ear to my words." Acts of the Apostles 6:8, "[And] Stephen..." to chapter 8:2, "...great lamentation over him." Alleluia: "Strengthened by the Holy Spirit." Gospel: John 12:24, "Truly, truly, I say to you..." to verse 26, "...my Father."

On the Third Day in the morning, Tone 2 [ahg]: "Co-essential", with its proper service. Psalm, antiphon: Psalm 109 [110], antiphon: "In the beauty [of holiness]", response: "The Lord said to my Lord." Gospel: Matthew 3:1, "In those days..." to verse 11, [or rather, Matthew 4] "...and they ministered to him." Midday Introit: "Mother of God, unwedded." Psalm 2, antiphon: "The Lord said to me", response: "Why did the heathen rage?" Epistle: Hebrews 1:1, "In many parts..." to verse 12, "...and your years shall not fail." Alleluia: "John the Forerunner." Gospel: Matthew 2:13, "And when they departed..." to verse 23, "...he shall be called a Nazarene."

On the Fourth Day in the morning, Tone 3 [ahf]: "Today Gabriel", with its proper service. Gospel: John 1:1, "In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was..." to verse 17, "...came through Jesus Christ."

Midday Entry-Hymn: "We confess you, O Mother of God" [Asduadzadzin zkez].
Psalm 109 [110], Antiphon: "In holy splendor." Epistle: Galatians 4:1, "Now I say... to verse 7... and an heir through God." Alleluia: "Rejoice, O highly favored." Gospel: Luke 1:26, "Now in the sixth month... to verse 38... and the angel departed from her."

On the Fifth Day of the Theophany: Morning Tone 3 [ahg - gyuz]: "Today He who is on the throne" [Aysor vor yator], with its canon. Gospel: John 1:18, "No one has seen God at any time; the only begotten... to verse 28... was baptizing, and he was there."
Midday Entry-Hymn: "We beseech you" [Aghachemkk zkez]. Psalm 98 [99], Antiphon: "Exalt the Lord." Response: "The Lord reigns, let the peoples tremble." Epistle: Hebrews 12:17 [12:18], "For you have not come... to verse 27... so that those things which cannot be shaken may remain." Alleluia: "And you, child, will be called the prophet." Gospel: Luke 1:39, "Now Mary arose... to verse 56... and returned to her house."

On the Sixth Day of the Theophany: Morning Tone 2 [ahf - pyur]: "Without beginning" [Anasgbanagan], with its canon. Gospel: John 1:29, "The next day John saw... to verse 34... This is the Son of God."
Midday Entry-Hymn: "O Mother of God, unwedded" [Asduadzadzin anharsnatsyal]. Psalm 29 [30], Antiphon: "O Lord, You brought me up." Response: "I will extol You." Epistle: 1 Thessalonians 4:12 [4:13], "But I do not want you to be ignorant, brethren... to verse 18... with these words." Alleluia: "He brought me out." Gospel: John 11:1, "Now a certain man was sick... to verse 46... and told them what He had done."

On the Seventh Day of the Theophany: Morning Tone 3 [ahg - pyur]: "Incomprehensible" [Aneghaneli], with its canon. Gospel: John 1:35, "Again, the next day, John stood... to verse 51... upon the Son of Man."
Midday Entry-Hymn: "We confess you, O Mother of God" [Asduadzadzin zkez khostovanyal]. Psalm 97 [98], Antiphon: "Proclaim the good news." Response: "Sing to the Lord." Epistle: Romans 1:1, "Paul, a bondservant of Jesus Christ... to verse 7... and from the Lord Jesus Christ." Alleluia: "The Lord has sworn." Gospel: Luke 2:1, "And it came to pass in those days... to verse 7... room in the inn."

The Eighth Day is the Feast of the Birth and Circumcision of our Lord Jesus Christ. Morning Tone 4 [ahf - dyu]: "Light from Light" [Loys i luso], with its canon. After "The Lord from heaven" [Ter hergnitsn], without psalm, is said: "The Son." Gospel: Luke 2:8, "Now there were shepherds..." up to the point where it says: "Glory to God in the highest."
As on the night of the Nativity, they shall say the first verse of the "Glory in the highest," and then read the remainder of the Gospel: Luke 2:15, "And it came to pass, when they had gone... to verse 20... as it had been told them." And they shall say the other verse: "And at all times." Morning Song: "Sun of Righteousness" [Aregagn artarutyan]. Psalm 109 [110], Antiphon: "In holy splendor." Response: "The Lord said to my Lord." Gospel: Luke 3:1, "Now in the fifteenth year... to Luke 4:13... for a season."
Midday Entry-Hymn: "We beseech you" [Aghachemkk zkez]. Psalm 97 [98], Antiphon: "The Lord showed." Response: "Bless the Lord." Epistle: Colossians 2:8, "Beware lest... to verse 15... by His resurrection." Alleluia: "Rejoice, O highly favored." Gospel: Luke 2:21, "And when eight days were completed... to verse 21... in the womb."
But in the evening, the [Hambartsi] Tone 3 [ahg]: "Light from Light" [Loys i luso], say the three together.
Afterwards, Litany: "Holy Mother of God" [Surp zasduadzadzin]. Prayer: "Receive, Lord, our supplications" [Yngal der zagachans].

From here until the Eve of the Fast of Catechumens [Arachavori], always consult the Calendar of the year to find the duration of the intervening period, and concerning the upcoming feasts, how and where they ought to be celebrated. Likewise for the Sundays, Wednesdays, and Fridays of these weeks, together with their appropriate [Hargh] hymns, the Year-Letter will indicate them to you; you shall see the pattern here.

The First Sunday after the Octave:
Tone ... [Hargh] Tone 3 [ahg - gyuz]: "The dawning of the Only-Begotten" [Dakumyn miadzni]. Morning Psalm 112 [113], Antiphon: "Blessed be the name of the Lord." Response: "Praise, O servants." Gospel: Luke 4:14, "Then Jesus returned... to verse 30... passing through the midst of them, He went His way." Litany: "Let us ask." Prayer: "Your all-powerful."
Midday Psalm 64 [65]: "Praise is due to You." Prophet: Isaiah 54:1, "Sing, O barren... to verse 13... your children." Epistle: 1 Timothy 1:1, "Paul, an apostle of Jesus Christ... to verse 11... committed to my trust." Alleluia: "Of the Resurrection." Gospel: John 2:1, "On the third day... to verse 11... His disciples."

First Wednesday. Midday Psalm 85 [86], Antiphon: "Preserve Your servant." Response: "Have mercy on me." Epistle: 1 Timothy 2:8, "I desire therefore..."

Canon of the Forty-Day Coming of Christ our God into the Temple [Presentation / Candlemas]:

Now, the feast of the Forty-Day Coming of Christ into the Temple is placed here, which is called "The Lord's Presentation" [Tyarn'ndaraj], and is always celebrated on February 14; which you must perform as follows:
On the 13th day of February, in the evening, first say: "Bow down [your ear]" and "Save [me]."
"Gladsome Light" [Phos Hilaron].
Gradual [Mesedi] of the Coming: "Blessed is he who comes in the name of the Lord; the Lord God has appeared to us."
Litany: "Let us say."
"Holy God," who came and art to come.
Entrance Hymn Tone 1 [ah]: "Incarnate from the Virgin."
Afterward, the Litany: "For peace."
Prayer: "Compassionate Father."
And the priests shall vest together, and they shall go out with the cross, the gospel, and all ornaments to the portal, singing the hymn Tone 7 [bg]: "Today, the unoriginated birth..." with its proper verses.
And at the conclusion of the hymn, Psalm 97 [98]: "All the ends have seen..."
Response: "Sing to the Lord."
Reading, Leviticus 12:6: "And the Lord said to Moses..." to verse 8: "...and she shall be cleansed."
Proverbs 8:22: "The Lord created me..." to verse 24: "...who keeps my ways."
Prophet Ezekiel 44:1: "And he brought me back..." to verse 2: "...and it shall be shut."
Prophet Malachi 3:1: "Thus says the Lord..." to verse 4: "...and as in former years."
Epistle Galatians 3:24: "Therefore the law was our tutor..." to verse 29: "...heirs according to the promise."
Alleluia: "Blessed is he who is to come."
Gospel Luke 2:22: "And when [the days] were fulfilled..." to verse 40: "...was upon him."
Litany: "For the peace from above," and they shall bless the four corners of the world [Antasdan].
Litany: "By this holy cross."
Prayer: "Protect [us]."
And they shall light the fire, and they shall sing the canticles: "Christ of glory..."
And then: "Save [us]."
And they shall be dismissed.
(Note: The practice of walking around the fire is objectionable, which was introduced by the ignorant.)

But if it happens to fall during the Fast of Catechumens [Yarajavorats],
or during the Great Lent, on February 13, at the Midday Hour:
they shall say the three "Have Mercy" [Oghormya] hymns according to the order of the day,
and they shall read the Scripture [books] of the day;
they shall say the "For the Souls" [Hogvots] prayer and be dismissed,
omitting [the prayers] "Bow Down [Your Ear]" (Khonarhetso) and "Save [Us]" (Apretso).
Then in the evening, "Bow Down [Your Ear]" (Khonarhetso) and "Save [Us]" (Apretso) and so on, according to the above-written instruction.
And during the night, which is February 14,
there is a vigil with services and psalms.
And the Holy Sacrament is placed upon a table in the chancel;
and all, having vested, shall come into the chancel,
and they shall say the processional [hetevak] hymn in Tone 3 [gd]: "Who became incarnate from the Virgin" (Marmnatsyald i kusen).
And the Blessing [Orhnutiun] hymn in Tone 4 [dad]: "Today, without beginning" (Aysor anskzbnakan).
Alleluia. "Blessed is He who comes" and "The Lord our God."
And then they shall read the four Gospels in the four corners of the church.

The first Gospel they shall read facing the East.
Hymn, Tone 1 [ats]: "Who became incarnate" (Marmnatsyald), "Rejoice greatly" (Urakh ler yoyzh), "Image-sharing" (Patkerakits).
After which: "Stand [firm]" (Ortiu). Gospel according to Matthew 5:17–20: "Do not think that..." to "...into the kingdom of heaven."

The second facing the West side.
Hymn, Tone 4 [dz]: "Today, without beginning" (Aysor anskzbnakan), "Today having come to the temple" (Aysor i tacharn ekial).
After which: "Stand [firm]" (Ortiu). Gospel according to Mark 13:32–37: "But about that day..." to "...keep watch."

The third facing the South side.
Hymn, Tone 4 [dz]: "Light from Light" (Loys i lusoy), "You who [by] Spirit" (Zkez vor hogi).
After which: "Stand [firm]" (Ortiu). Gospel according to Luke 12:49–59: "I came to bring fire..." to "...the very last penny."

And the fourth facing the North side.
Hymn, Tone 4 [ek]: "Today in the temple" (Aysor i tacharn), "In the beginning was the Word" (I skzbane bann).
After which: "Stand [firm]" (Ortiu). Gospel according to John 1:1–17: "In the beginning was the Word..." to "...came through Christ."

After which, hymn, Tone 2 [bk]: "At the coming of the Lord" (I galstean tearn).
And then coming into the chancel they shall say the "Fathers" [Harts] hymn with its canons.
And if it happens on a Sunday,
read the Myrrhbearers [Yughaberits] Gospel.
Song: "Sun of Justice" (Aregakn ardarutean).
Litany: "Holy Mother" (Mayr surb). "Holy God."

And then: "For the Souls" [Requiem].
Again, know this: that when the letter of the year is "Alis" [letter Ա], the eight-day octave of the Nativity of Christ takes two days from the fast. And if the letter of the year is "E" [letter Ը], it takes one day from the fast, during which, although the feast of the Nativity is celebrated with services and Liturgy, yet the fast is unbreakable. Furthermore, from here until the Great Barekendan [Pre-Lenten Sunday], although feasts are set as immovable, yet you must not let the annual calendar out of your hands.

Saturday: of the holy saints Sarkis the General, and his son Martiros, and his fourteen soldiers. Tone 1 [ah, ak]: "To the All-Holy Trinity" (Amenasurp errorduteann). Father of the Tone [Harts] Tone 6 [gk]: "The Soldiership" (Zinvorutiunk). Magnificat [Mankunk] Tone 1 [ad]: "The Soldier of Christ" (Zinavorn kristosi). Midday Entrance Hymn: "Soldier" (Zorakan).

Know here: that after the first Sunday of the Nativity, the Sunday of the Barekendan of the Fast of Catechumens [Arachavorats], and for this Sunday, the Father of the Tone [Harts] hymns, which are: Tone 4 [dg]: "The Dawning of the Only-Begotten" (Tsagumn miadsni); Tone 5 [gdz]: "With Glory" (Paravorelov); and Tone 8 [dk]: "You, the Word without beginning" (Anskizbn band)—which were placed as immovable—yet their immobility lasts from the 20th letter of the year until the 36th [K] letter of the year. But from the 1st letter of the year to the 10th letter of the year, this rule is not kept. Therefore, in such years, you must look at the letter of the year, and according to their indication, say these Father of the Tone [Harts] hymns of these three Sundays.

And drawing the curtain, they shall cover the altars, in the likeness of the expulsion of Adam from paradise, and they shall not kiss the Gospel henceforth; neither shall they raise [it] during the "We believe" [Creed] and during "Save, Lord" [Ketso] for the congregation to see; also, they shall say the "Joyful Light" [Phos Hilaron] by number [recited without chant] in place of the supplication until Palm Sunday.
--[Separator]--
On the Sunday of the True Eve of Great Lent [Bun Barekendan]:
Change the tone at the end to Tone 6 [gk]. But the first canon is "Confess [the Lord]" and the last canon is "In my distress."
Canon Leader: "Bless the Lord, O my soul; I will praise the Lord in my life, I will sing psalm to my God as long as I exist."
Say the exchange [verse] in full number, and at the end in chant:
"I will bless You with thanksgiving in my life, I will sing psalm to You, Christ the King, with a new victorious song of praise." Repeat thrice.
Then by number: "Praise the Lord, for a psalm is good, let praise be sweet to our God." Say the exchange in full, then in chant:
"May the thanksgivings of those who fear Your name be sweet to You, O Christ, which You have made them hope for by Your mercy in the mysteries of Your life-giving coming." Repeat thrice.
Again by number: "Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem." Say the exchange in full, then in chant:
"Your holy redeemed church praises You, O Christ; rejoicing, it sees You, the Savior, coming in Fatherly authority to crown Your saints." Repeat thrice.
Then: "Today ineffable [the hymn]."
Psalm: "A sign was shown [Psalm 4:6]."
Gospel of the Crucifixion; and one verse of the song from "The Renewer" [Norogogh], and then "For the Souls" [Hogvots]; for during Great Lent, there is no Rest Hymn [Hangstean Sharakan] nor "God ineffable."
Morning Hymn Tone 8 [dk]: "The Word Who with the Father," with its canon.
Myrrh-bearing [oil-bearing] Gospel: Matthew 27:57: "And when it was evening... to verse 61: ...opposite the tomb."
On the Sundays of Great Lent, read only [the passage of] the Burial from the Myrrh-bearing Gospel. After this, say three times:
"All the days of my life, I will not cease blessing You, Christ, Savior of the world," but the last time aloud. Say this until Easter Sunday.
And then say "In the night" Tone 8 [de].
Morning Gospel: Matthew 6:22: "The lamp of the body is the eye... to [7:27]: ...and great was its fall."
And say only the first two stanzas of "Newly Created" [Norasteghdzeal]. Afterward: "Save, Lord" [Ketso], without the Gospel. This is the order of the Sundays of Lent.
At Midday [Liturgy]:
Psalm 64 [65]: "Praise is due to You."
Prophet: Isaiah 58:1: "Cry aloud with power... to verse 14: ...[the mouth of the Lord] has spoken this."
Psalm 75 [76]: "Make vows." Verse: "In Judah is [God] known."
The Apostle: Romans 13:11: "And know this... to 14:25 [16:27]: ...glory forever, Amen."
Alleluia Psalm 99 [100]: "Shout joyfully to the Lord," say in full.
Gospel: Matthew 6:1: "Take heed to your almsgiving... to verse 21: ...there your heart will be also."
On these Sundays of Lent, at the Midday Liturgy, say the first Blessing [Orhnutiun] hymn, and the last one at the Evening Hambartsi [Hymn of Ascent]; and at the Divine Liturgy, the Rest Hymn, "Profound Mystery" [Khorhurd Khorin], and "In This Sanctuary of Offerings" do not take place.
Moreover, from here until the first Sunday of the Coming of the Holy Spirit [Pentecost], all the established items—namely feasts, Patristic [Harts] hymns, intermediate periods, and tones—remain always fixed, except for the Presentation of the Lord [Tiarn'ndaraj] and the Annunciation, and the daily tones are also indicated by the daily Blessing [Orhnutiun] hymns, as you see.
--[Separator]--
On the first Monday of the Fast:
Hymn of Repentance [Harts], say in order, Tone 1 [ad]: "Who lookest with sweetness," but do not say the "At-the-Dawn" [Aghotsm] hymns.
Midday Mesedi: Psalm 50 [51]: "Turn [Your face] from me, Lord." Verse: "Have mercy on me, O God."
Prophet: Isaiah 1:16: "Wash yourselves, make yourselves clean... to verse 20: ...[the mouth of the Lord] has spoken this."
On these two days at Midday, say only the "Have Mercy" [Voghormia] hymns, as is customary.

And [the Gospel] finished. Gospel according to Matthew 20:29 "And as they went out..." to 21:47 "and he lodged there."
Hymn of Hallowing: "Angelic" (Hreshtakayin).
But at the ninth hour, they shall assemble at the door of the church. Psalm 97 [98] "Let the mountains rejoice." Proverbs 8:22 "The Lord created me..." to verse 31 "...with the sons of men." Prophet Jeremiah (Baruch) 4:36 "Look toward the east..." to 5:4 "...and the glory of God's worship." 1 Peter Catholic Epistle 1:3 "Blessed be God..." to verse 9 "...the salvation of souls." Alleluia: "The Lord is in His temple." Gospel according to Luke 19:29 "And it came to pass, when he drew near..." to verse 48 "...to hear Him."
They shall go out in procession with Cross, Gospel, and all adornments, and they shall begin the hymn in Tone 3 [b3/tsa] "You Who are praised above..." and at its completion, "Stand upright" (Ort'iwn). Gospel according to Matthew 21:1 "And when they drew near..." to verse 10 "...Hosanna in the highest." Hymn in Tone 3 [gdz/ga] "Being King." After this: "Stand upright" (Ort'iwn). Gospel according to Mark 11:1 "And when they came near..." to verse 10 "...and glory in the highest." Hymn in Tone 4 [ddz/da] "When Jesus came." After this: "Stand upright" (Ort'iwn). Gospel according to Luke 18:31 "Then He took the twelve..." to 19:28 "...going before, ascending to Jerusalem." Hymn in Tone 4 [ddz/da] "Your coming, O Christ." After this: "Stand upright" (Ort'iwn). Gospel according to John 12:12 "On the next day..." to verse 23 "...the Son of Man." Hymn in Tone 3 [gdz/ga] "In the resurrection." After this, Psalm 4 "A sign was set..." Antiphon: "When I called." Gospel according to Matthew 24:27 "For as the lightning..." to verse 35 "...and my words shall not pass away." And they shall sign the fields with the cross [Andastan]. Litany: "By this Holy Cross." Prayer: "Guardian" [Pahpanich].
And then they shall come to the porch [narthex], holding the door of the church closed. They shall begin the "Bow down" [Khonarhuts'o] (Psalm 85 [86]), six verses, but the "Save me" [Aprets'o] (Psalm 139 [140]) one by one in alternation, as they are arranged: "Bow down, O Lord." "Preserve my soul." "Be merciful to me, O Lord." "For You, Lord." "Give ear, O Lord." "In the day of my trouble." Hymn in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "Rejoice," repeat three times.
"There is none like..." "All nations..." "Great are You..." "Lead me..." "I will confess..." "Great was..." Hymn in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "Rejoice greatly," repeat three times.
"O God, the lawless..." "But You, Lord..." "Give strength..." "Let them see, my haters..." Hymn in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "Be glad," repeat three times.
Glory to the Father and... Now and always. And then, Petition: "Glory to You, O God." Litany: "Again and again in peace." Blessing and glory.
"Deliver me, O Lord." "Who devise mischief." "They have sharpened their tongues." "Keep me, O Lord." "Who have purposed to overthrow." "They have hidden a snare." "I said to the Lord, You are my God." "O Lord, the strength." "Do not grant, O Lord." "Lest they exalt themselves." "As for the head." "Let burning coals fall." "Let not an evil speaker." "I know." "Surely the righteous shall give thanks." Hymn in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "Christ the King," repeat three times.
"Lord, I cry." "Let my prayer be set forth." "Set a watch, O Lord." "In practicing wicked works." "Let the righteous smite me." "Our bones are scattered." "But my eyes are unto You." "Keep me from the snare." "Let the wicked fall into their own nets." Hymn in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "Go forth to meet Him," repeat three times.
"I cried unto the Lord with my voice." "I poured out my complaint before Him." "When my spirit was overwhelmed within me." "I looked on my right hand." "I cried unto You, O Lord." "Attend unto my cry." "Bring my soul out of prison." "The righteous shall compass me about." Hymn in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "In unity," repeat three times.
Glory to the Father and the Son. Now and always and. Petition: "Blessed is the Lord, Who dwells." Mesedi: Of the Coming. "Holy God... Who came and are to come." Evening Hymn [Hambartsi] in Tone 2 [bk/pake] "Today with the fiery-formed." Litany: "For peace." Prayer: "Merciful Father."
After "Merciful Father," one of the venerable priests shall vest and come to the door of the church, and shall begin in a loud voice Psalm 117 [118] "Blessed is He that comes." Antiphon: "O give thanks..." up to that place which says: "Open to me the gates of righteousness." And then they shall say the Hymn in Tone 3 [b3/tsa] "Grant us, O Lord." And at its completion, the vested priest shall begin to say in a sorrowfully pleading voice—since it contains the mystery of the Second Coming and the Day of Judgment:
"Open to us, O Lord; open to us, O Lord; open to us, O Lord, the gate of mercy, as we cry unto You weeping."
And one of the priests from within, answering, shall say:

## page_0473_auto

"Who are these, that I may open? For this is the door of the Lord, and the righteous enter through it."
And the priest from without shall say:
"Not only do the righteous enter, but also sinners, justified through confession and repentance, enter through it."
From within, he shall say:
"For this is the door of heaven and the valley of sorrow, which God covenanted to Jacob; rest for the righteous, expiation for sinners, the royal palace of Christ, the dwelling place of angels, the assembly of the saints, the place of refuge and house of God."
The Priest from without:
"True and worthy are those things you say concerning this holy church; for she is our immaculate mother, and from her we are born as children of light and truth; and she is our hope of life, and through her we find salvation of souls; and because she is our path of righteousness, through her we ascend to Christ our heavenly Father."
And they shall say the hymn [Sharakan] tone 2nd plagal [Bki - բկ]: "Wise Virgins" (Imastun Kusankn). "My Hope" (Hoys im). "My Sins" (Meghq im).
And at the completion, the priest from without shall begin:
"Open to us, O Lord, open to us, O Lord, open to us, O Lord, the door of mercy, we who call upon You with lamentation."
From within, he shall say:
"Behold, we see our Lord Christ, who is seated upon the holy altar, and calls to Himself His willing ones, saying: 'Where I am, there also my servant shall be.'"
The Priest from without:
"We are the price of the incorruptible blood of the Son of God; with joy let us bless Christ our Savior, and let us all say: Glory to Your awesome coming, O Lord."
Hymn [Sharakan] tone 2nd class [B3 - բձ]: "King of Glory, to You."
The Priest from without:
"Open to us, O Lord, open to us, O Lord, open to us, O Lord, the door of mercy, we who call upon You with lamentation."
From within:
"The herald of the awesome God cries out with a formidable voice, saying: 'Behold, the bridegroom comes, go out to meet him.'"
And the priest from without, with fear and trembling, shall say:
"Behold, I and my children whom God gave to me, we all stand and await the sight of Christ, and to hear His blessed voice."
From within:
"The voice of God the Creator cries out, saying: 'Come, blessed of My Father, inherit the kingdom prepared for you from the beginning of the world.'"
And then the priest from without shall say:
"Open to me the gates of righteousness, that I may enter through them and give thanks [to the Lord]."
And they shall open the door, and all the people shall enter into the church with spiritual joy.
And they shall begin the hymn [Sharakan] tone 3rd plagal [Gki - գկ]: "Open to us the door of Your mercy." And then: "Orthos" [Stand].
Gospel: John 12:12 "The next day..." to verse 23 "...that the Son of Man may be glorified." Read this Gospel on the bim [altar stage].
Litany [Qaroz]: "Let us ask..." "Save [us]." Prayer: "King of Peace."

Great Monday. Hymn tone 2nd class [B3 - բձ]: "Uncreated from the Father," with its canons.
Morning song: "From the beginning, Lord." Response [Phokh]: "They shall perish."
Litany [Qaroz]: "Let us glorify." "Holy God," [with the clause] "who came and art to come."
After the Canticle of the Youths [Mankunq], Litany [Qaroz]: "For peace." Prayer: "Holy art Thou, Lord."
After that, Psalm 103 [104], in a mourning tone: "Confession and majesty..." Response [Phokh]: "Bless the Lord, O my soul."

And say the "Creator of All" [Ararchakan] as appropriate.

Also, the supplication [Maghtanq] of the Coming is said here until Friday.
But on these three days, there is no Sunrise service canon, nor the "Lord Almighty" [Ter Amenakal], nor the Liturgy.

And at the third hour, they shall gather at the church door for the dismissal of the penitents; whether there be any or not, the service must be performed.

Hymn [sharakan]: "And your Holy Spirit" [Yev zhogit surb], three times. Again: "Lord Almighty." Hymn [sharakan]: "And save me" [Yev prkya zis], three times. "We beseech and request." And afterward the Litany: "We who have come to confession" [Yekyalqs i khostovan]. Prayer: "Lord God of our salvation."

And the deacon shall proclaim the litany: "May the Lord hear the voice of our supplications, almighty Lord our God, save and have mercy." And say three times: "Lord have mercy," and then read the prayer of Saint Basil: "Lord God, Father Almighty."
After the prayers, Psalm 117 [118]: "Open to me [the gates of righteousness]," response: "Give thanks [to the Lord]," up to that place where it says: "Open to me."
And entering into the church, they shall begin the hymn [sharakan] in Tone Plagal Fourth [dk]: "The door of your mercy" [Zoghormutyan ko zdurn]. And upon its completion, Psalm 99 [100]: "Make a joyful noise unto the Lord," say in full.
Afterward the Litany: "Through this holy church." Prayer: "In the midst of this temple."
And then the bishop or the senior priest shall read the prayer of blessing over the penitents. Afterward the Litany: "Let us ask." And he shall say thirty times: "Lord have mercy." Prayer: "To your almighty [power]."
And they shall place the holy cross and the Gospel book before the altar, and they shall kiss them, and they shall give the four-drachma coin, which Moses commanded for the Levites. And the priest shall bless the penitents and dismiss them.
And at the sixth hour they shall assemble in the church, they shall cover the altar, and they shall say the remaining mercy-psalms [Oghormya]. And then they shall begin: "Incline [your ear]" and "Save [me]" [Psalms 85 and 142].

And while they read this, the celebrant shall go up to the altar and vest, and he shall veil the mystery until the "We believe" [the Creed]. Litany: "Let us beseech." "Holy God, who didst come and art to come." [Hymn at the] Evening hour Tone 1 [ak]: "Today was erected." Litany: "For peace." Prayer: "Compassionate Father." Afterward: Psalm 22 [23]: "Thou hast prepared." Response: "The Lord shall shepherd." The clerks shall come up vested and come before the altar, and they shall read the books.
The Apostle: 1 Corinthians 11:23: "For I received," to verse 32: "condemned." Alleluia: "Thou hast prepared." Gospel according to Mark [Matthew] 26:17: "And on the first day," to verse 30: "to the Mount of Olives." The deacon shall read this at the altar. Then they shall open the altar, and say the "We believe" [the Creed]. Song of Holiness [Trisagion/Sanctus]: "Who sittest." And when the celebrant reaches that place where he says: "And grant us with a bold voice," the chief priest shall go up to the bema holding the cross and the Gospel in his hand, and stretching forth his hand, he shall declare to the people the word of Saint Basil in the hearing of all. First with terrifying words, and then in an encouraging way, saying: "Hearken unto me, my children." And when it is finished, they shall begin "Our Father" with a loud voice. And when "Christ sacrificed" [the Communion hymn] is completed, the clerks shall say in an encouraging tone this Tagh [canticle]: "Come, all ye people." And then the worthy shall communicate, and they shall be dismissed.

But in the evening at the tenth hour:
They shall assemble in the narthex of the church and perform the Order of the Washing of the Feet. But having prepared beforehand the vessels for the mystical washing, and they shall place the holy thing upon the cushions, and they shall come into the chancel vested, and they shall begin with a mournful voice Psalm 50 [51]: "Sprinkle me with hyssop" (Psalm 50:7 [51:7]), Response: "Have mercy on me, O God" (Psalm 50:1 [51:1]).
Afterward, Hymn Tone 1 [ak]: "Today was erected." And at the end, the deacon shall proclaim the litany: "And again thanksgiving." And then fifty "Lord, have mercy" by count. And at the completion, the bishop or the priest shall read this prayer: "We glorify Thee." And then taking water, he shall pour it into the basin in the form of a cross, saying in a [chanting] voice Psalm 28 [29]: "The voice of the Lord is upon the waters," Response: "Bring to the Lord, ye sons of God."

And then the discourse written by the blessed John the Doctor [Hovhannes Vardapet] on love, which says: "God is love." And at the end, Alleluia: "Thou hast prepared before me... Thou didst anoint with oil." Gospel according to John 13:1: "Before the feast," to verse 11: "but not all."
Litany, chanted: "Great and wonderful-working God."
Afterward, Tagh [canticle] of the Apostle-Doctor.
And at the completion, they shall begin to read this Prayer over the oil and the water: "Our Lord Jesus Christ." And at the end, they shall sign with the sign of the cross and with the Gospel the oil and the water.
Afterward, Litany: "By this holy cross." Prayer: "Guard us."

And then they shall vest the bishop or one of the honorable priests, who, taking the apron, shall sit on his knees and wash the feet of all, from the greatest to the least. And the others, standing in ranks on this side and on that side, shall sing "He who stretched out the heavens" with a sweet voice, and "Today, ineffable." And those who have been washed shall kiss the Gospel and the holy cross and the right hand of the priest. And after the washing: Orthi [Stand up], Gospel according to John 13:12: "So after..."

On Great Friday, in the night there is a vigil, which is the fulfillment of all that is written.
Canon-heading, Tone 7 [dz]: "Bless the Lord, O my soul." Follower Hymn, Tone 7 [dz/ahg]: "Today in fulfillment."
Afterwards: Litany, "Again and peace." "Blessing and glory."
And then they shall light twelve candles upon the altar on this side and on that side, and one large candle in the middle.
And two priests shall say three alternating psalms.

First Psalm: Psalm 2, "Why did the nations rage?"; Psalm 3, "Lord, why are they multiplied?"; Psalm 4, "When I called."
And the others shall sing aloud: "The rulers of the people gathered together against the Lord and against his anointed." They shall repeat it three times, but the last time aloud; on the other six verses as well.
Afterward, the Song of Lord Nerses, spoken concerning Great Friday: "Today Ineffable," until the place where it says: "I will fulfill the law."
And at the completion, from the rank of the senior priests: "Stand up."
Gospel according to John 13:16, "Amen, I say to you, a servant is not greater," to verse 18:1, "himself and his disciples."
Prayer with kneeling: "Let us beseech the Lord," three times.
And at the completion of each single Gospel, they shall extinguish two candles, one from the right and one from the left of the twelve candles, starting from the last ones unto the first.

Second Psalm: Psalm 40 [41], "Blessed is he who considers the poor"; Psalm 41 [42], "As the hart pants"; Psalm 42 [43], "Judge me, O God."
Refrain aloud: "An unjust word they laid against me, Lord, Lord, do not leave me," three times.
Hymn: "I will fulfill the law of Moses."
Afterward: "Stand up."
Gospel according to Luke 22:1, "Now the feast drew near," to verse 65, "they spoke against him."
Prayer with kneeling, three times.

Third Psalm: Psalm 58 [59], "Deliver me from my enemies"; Psalm 59 [60], "O God, you have rejected [us]"; Psalm 60 [61], "Hear, O God, my prayer."
Refrain aloud: "Deliver me from my enemies, O my God, and from my persecutors save me," three times.
Hymn: "With the care of love."
Afterward: "Stand up."
Gospel according to Mark 14:27, "And he said to them," to verse 72, "and he began to weep."

Fourth Psalm: Psalm 78 [79], "O God, the nations have entered"; Psalm 79 [80], "O Shepherd [of Israel]"; Psalm 80 [81], "Sing aloud to God."
Refrain aloud: "They were rejected by your hand, we are your people and the sheep of your pasture," three times.
Hymn: "Ray of Light."
Afterward: "Stand up."
Gospel according to Matthew 26:31, "Then Jesus said to them," to verse 56, "and they fled."

Fifth Psalm: Psalm 108 [109], "O God of my praise"; Psalm 109 [110], "The Lord said to my Lord"; Psalm 110 [111], "I will give thanks to you, Lord, with my whole [heart]."
Refrain aloud: "They spoke against me with a lying tongue, and with words of hatred they surrounded me," three times.
Hymn: "With evil bonds."
Afterward: "Stand up."
Gospel according to Matthew 26:47 [Note: 26:57], "But those who had seized Jesus," to verse 75, "he wept bitterly."

Sixth Psalm: Psalm 117 [118], "Give thanks to the Lord, for...", until the place: "Open to me the gates."
Refrain aloud: "Give thanks to the Lord, for he is good, for his mercy endures forever," three times.
Hymn: "Spread out."
Afterward: "Stand up."
Gospel according to John 18:2, "And Judas knew," to verse 27, "and immediately the cock crowed."
This Gospel they shall read at the Chancel.
And then, Petition: "Of the souls."
Litany: "For the souls of the departed."
Prayer: "Christ, Son of God."

Canon-heading: "Princes persecuted me," in a mournful voice.
Afterwards: "Again and peace." "Blessing and glory to the Father."
Blessing Hymn, Tone 3 [be/ahf]: "With love of money," with its steps [sarok’n].
Alleluia: "You did mount your horses." "I was betrayed and did not."
But when they reach the "Lord of heaven" hymn, they shall hide the one large remaining candle until "Glory in the highest" is completed, and then they shall bring it out.

Morning Song: "I was betrayed and did not come forth." "I cried to."
Litany: "Let us glorify."
"Holy God," who wast betrayed.
After "Holy and Lord," Psalm 108 [109] with a slow melody. Read the alternating verses in a mournful voice: "In return for my love they betrayed me," alternate: "O God of my praise."

And then, placing the holy Cross and the Gospel upon the lectern, and the priests forming a circle, the bishop or the senior priest shall cense, and the others shall begin the Hymn Tone 2 [Pyts]: "We bow down before Your Cross, O Christ, and we magnify Your holy betrayal, and we glorify Your holy sufferings. Holy God, holy and mighty, holy and immortal, who wast betrayed for us, have mercy on us" — repeating this three times.
And then: "Glory to Your holy Cross, Alleluia; to Your holy betrayal, Alleluia; to Your sufferings, Alleluia." "It was signed..." etc. "Glory to Your holy Cross..." and so on likewise. "Glory and worship to the Father and the Son..." etc. "Glory to Your holy Cross..." and so on likewise.
Then: "The life-giving Cross which became salvation for us." Afterward, Litany: "By this holy Cross..." Prayer: "Keep us..."
And then they shall read the Testament of Great Friday, which is concerning the Crucifixion of Christ. On this day during the daytime, there is no command to kneel, until the evening hour.
And at the third hour of the day, they shall come and say the three "Have mercy on me" [psalms] together with the great Litanies and Prayers. Afterward: "For the souls of those who are asleep."

Then at the sixth hour, they shall assemble at the door of the church, and they shall perform the Order of the Crucifixion.
They shall begin to read the eight alternating Psalms, and the sixteen scriptural readings, and the four Gospel chapters. The first reading belongs to the class of the disciples.

[On the Sabbath]:
Song: "The Lord after creation" (Tērn yet steghtsmann). Litany: "Let us ask" (Kndrests`uk`). Prayer: "To Your all-powerful and wondrous burial." Midday Liturgy Introit: "Christ is risen from the dead." Response: "Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem." Hymn Tone 1 [ats]: "Christ is risen from the dead." No vigil. And they shall place the Holy Sacrament [relics/vessels] upon the cushion-stand in the chancel.

And the priests shall vest together and come into the chancel, and begin the Hymn of the Day, Tone 1 [ahz]: "Today He rose." Canticle, Tone 8 [deg]: "Today great good news," with its accompanying verses.
The Myrrh-bearing Gospel, John, the last portion: 20:1, "And on the first day of the week," to verse 18, "she said to him."
During this fifty-day period of Pentecost on Sundays, the Myrrh-bearing Gospel: read the last portion of the three chapters, and that of Matthew, read the same. But when they say the last verse of the "Praise the Lord from the heavens," everyone shall kiss one another, saying: "Christ is risen from the dead. Great tidings [good news] to you and to us!"
Song: "The Resurrection." Litany: "Let us all say." "Holy God, who rose from the dead." Until the Ascension, the Song and the "Holy God" are these.

And the priests shall vest together and go out in procession with the Cross and the Gospel, with incense and candles and all adornment, singing the hymn, Tone 1 [ahz]: "Today He rose." Psalm 64 [65]: "To you is due praise." Acts 1:1, "The first book," to verse 8, "of the earth." Alleluia: "They were standing." Gospel, Mark 16:2, "And early in the morning," to verse 8, "for they were afraid."

And then they shall sing the hymn, and read the four Gospels facing the four corners of the world. First: hymn, Tone 1 [ahz]: "With the dawning," facing the East. Stand [Orthi]. Gospel, John 19:38, "After this," to verse 42, "they laid Jesus." Facing the West, singing the hymn, Tone 2 [ak]: "You were sent from the Father." Gospel, Luke 23:50, "And behold, a man," to verse 56, "according to the commandment." Facing the South, singing the hymn, Tone 3 [bzh]: "At Your grave." Gospel, Mark 15:42, "And when evening came," to Mark 16:1, "they might come and anoint Him." And facing the North, singing the hymn, Tone 4 [bk]: "To Your resurrection." Gospel, Matthew 27:57, "And when evening came," to verse 66, "along with the guard."

And then they shall enter the chancel, singing the hymn, Tone 1 [ahz]: "Who Your immaculate arms." Then, Psalm 4: "There was signed," alternating with "In my calling." Gospel, John 19:16, "Then he delivered Him," to verse 22, "what I have written, I have written." Litany: "For the peace from above." And they shall bless the fields [Antasdan]. Litany: "By this Holy Cross." Prayer: "Protect [us]."

And then they shall begin the Introit: "For He is risen." Hymn, Tone 8 [dk]: "The Only-begotten Jesus," for the Mother of God, for the Cross, and "Glory." Psalm 129 [130]: "Let Your ears be attentive," alternating with "Out of the depths have I cried." Gospel, John 11:1, "Now there was a certain sick man," to verse 46, "what Jesus had done." Then they shall say the "For the Souls" [memorial prayers], and after that: Song, "Newly-created," say it fully. Litany...

On the Feast of the Holy Virgin Mary.
The Feast of the Annunciation of the Mother of God is placed here, which is celebrated on April 7. But in the evening, which is April 6, on whatever day it falls, after the proper [service] of the day, do the pre-feast service. Hymn Tone 2 [ak]: "The Ineffable Mystery," with its canon. After, Litany: "The Holy Mother of God." Prayer: "Receive, Lord."

And in the night, which is April 7, the priests shall vest together and come to the chancel, and begin the Morning Hymn Tone 2 [ak]: "The Ineffable Mystery," with its canon. Alleluia: of the Birth. Alleluia: "The Holy Spirit shall come." Gospel Luke 1:26 "And in the [sixth] month" to verse 38 "the angel [departed] from her." Litany: "Let us ask," "Save [us]." Prayer: "Your all-powerful." And if it falls on a Sunday of Lent, say the two stanzas of "Newly-created."

## page_0487_auto

If on other days: [sing] the Song of Creation here, and after it, "Hidden Mystery" (Dzadzkeal khorhourtn).
At the Midday Liturgy, Entrance Hymn (Zhamamout): "Mother of God, Unwedded" (Astuadzatsin anhars). Anthem (Pokh): "My soul magnifies" (Metsatsustse). Hymn Tone 6 [gki]: "Tabernacle of the Mother of God" (Astuadzatsin khoran).
The Psalm, Scripture Reading, and Gospel are those of the morning. Alleluia: "Rejoice, O favored one" (Urakh ler berkreal). Hymn of the Censing (Srbasatsutiun): "Multitude" (Bazmutiun).
But if it falls on Palm Sunday, or on Holy Thursday, or on Holy Friday, or on Holy Saturday, or on Easter Sunday: before the Blessing (Orhnutiun), extract the hymn "Ineffable Mystery" (Khorhourdn anchar) with its auxiliary hymns (sar), and then read the Scripture and the Gospel. Afterward, the Litany: "By the Holy Mother of God" (Surb zAstuadzatsinn). Prayer: "Receive, Lord" (Inkal ter). And then begin the proper service of the day.
And if it falls on the Saturday of Lazarus, or on Holy Monday, Holy Tuesday, or Holy Wednesday: before the Blessing, in place of the service hymn, extract the Blessing of the day along with its auxiliary hymns (sar). Read the Scripture of the day and the Gospel, and say the Song of Nerses and "For the Souls" (Hogvotsn). And then begin to perform the Feast of the Annunciation, saying the Blessing "Ineffable Mystery" (Khorhourdn anchar) according to the order, and the rest.
Do likewise if it falls during Eastertide (Yinank). But read the Gospel of the day after the Scripture and Gospel of the Annunciation; then "Save, O Lord" (Ketso).
Likewise, if it falls on Low Sunday (Ashkharhamatran Kiurake): after the Gospel of the Annunciation, read the Scripture and Gospel of the Antasdan [Blessing of the Fields], and perform the Antasdan. Then [read] the Gospel of the day, and "Newly Created" (Norasteghtseal) and "Save, O Lord" (Ketso).
But at the Midday Liturgy and in the Evening: the Psalms, Scriptures, and Gospels are of the Resurrection, etc. As for the Evening Hymn [Hambartsi]: on Saturday evening [sing] the Hymn of the Coming here, and on other days, that of the Annunciation, along with "Holy Mother" (Mayr surb).

**Second Sunday [GD], which is also called New Sunday (Krkynazatik) [Low Sunday].**
In the night, there is a Vigil. Canon head: "Bless the Lord, O my soul" (Orhneandzn im). Anthem (Pokh): "I will praise You with thanksgiving" (Orhnetsits zkez gohutyamp), as on the night of Great Barekendan. Night Hymn Tone 6 [gki]: "Renewed Islands" (Norogeal kghzik), with its auxiliary hymns (sar). Say the great Blessing after it.
Morning Psalm 84 [85]: "Show us, O Lord." Anthem (Pokh): "Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land." Until the Ascension, this is the morning Psalm.
Gospel: Luke 4:14 to verse 30: "he passed through the midst of them."
Afterward: "Newly Created" (Norasteghtseal). Litany: "Let us ask" (Khndresgouk). "Save, O Lord" (Ketso). Prayer: "To Your Almighty."
At the Midday Liturgy, Entrance Hymn (Zhamamout): "Christ is risen" (Kristos yareav). Until the Ascension, on every Sunday and Saturday, say "Christ is risen." But on other days, say "Awesome" (Ahavor) and "Since He rose" (Kanzi yareav).
Psalm 22 [23]: "The Lord is my shepherd." "The earth is the Lord's" [Psalm 23/24]. "Unto Thee, O Lord, do I lift up" [Psalm 24/25].
Acts 5:31 [or 5:17/34] to 6:7: "were obedient to the faith."
Psalm 64 [65]: "Praise waiteth for Thee."

## page_0496_human

Today the Apostles rejoice, adorned by the graces of the Holy Spirit.

## page_0497_human

CANON OF THE COMING OF THE HOLY SPIRIT

On the Sunday of the Coming of the Holy Spirit, which is called Pentecost, that is, the fiftieth day from Pascha. 
At night there is a vigil, and they shall place the Holy Sacrament upon the table of preparation. 
Processional hymn (tone [gd]): "Our Lord ascended into heaven" (Hambartsaw tērn mer yerkins). 
Hymn of blessing [ah.] Tone 4 [dz]: "The dove-like apostle" (Arakeloy aghawnwoy). 
First Alleluia: "A clean heart..." "Cast me not..." 
Response: "The wings of a dove covered with silver..." "When the heavenly King scattered..." 
Hymn of the Fathers [Harts], Tone 1 [ah]: "The Coming of the Spirit" (Hogwoyn zgaloustn), with its variables. 
Morning Song: "Let us bless the Coming of the Spirit" (Zgaloust hogwoyn orhnescouk), "Bless the Lord in the churches" (Yekegeghetsis orhnetsek). 
Litany: "Let us all say" (Asascouk amen). 
"Holy God," who didst come and rest in the apostles. 
During this week, this Song, Litany, and "Holy God" are used. 
And they shall go out in procession with the Cross and the Gospel and with all adornments, singing the hymn, Tone 4 [dz]: "The dove-like apostle" (Arakeloy aghawnwoy). 
Then Psalm 142 [143]. 
Antiphon: "Thy good and holy Spirit." 
Response: "Lord, hear my prayer." 
Acts 2:1: "And when the day... [to verse 21]... the Lord shall be saved." 
Alleluia, Psalm 142 [143]: "Thy good and holy Spirit." 
Gospel according to John 14:15: "If you love me... [to verse 24]... who sent me." 
Litany: "For the peace from above" (Vasn i veroust). 
And they shall sign the four corners of the world with the Cross [Andastan]. 
Litany: "By the Holy Cross" (Sourb khachivs). 
Prayer: "Guardian" (Pahpanea). 
And then they shall begin the hymn of rest in the proper tone. 
Psalm 129 [130]: "Let thine ears..." 
Response: "Out of the depths." 
Gospel according to Luke 12:49: "I am come to send fire... [to verse 59]... the very last mite." 
Then "Spirit of Souls" (Hogots). 
Litany: "For the souls of them that are asleep" (Vasn hangoutseal). 
Prayer: "Christ, Son of God." 
And then the hymn, Tone 2 [be]: "Indivisible Trinity" (Yerrordoutiun anbazhaneli). 
Then "Newly-created" (Norasteghtseal). 
Litany: "Let us ask" (Khndrescouk), "Save us" (Ketso). 
Prayer: "To Thy all-powerful and wondrous coming, O Holy Spirit and God, we bow down." 
Midday Introit: "We confess Thee, O God" (Astouats zkez khostovanimk). 
Psalm: "The Lord reigneth." 
Hymn, Tone 2 [by]: "Who from the ever-moving" (Or i harasharzh). 
Psalm 148: "Praise the Lord from the heavens," "Praise the Lord with a song," "Praise God," "I was small." 
Acts 2:1: "And when the day... [to verse 21]... the Lord shall be saved." 
Alleluia, Psalm 32 [33]: "By the mercy of the Lord." 
Gospel according to John 14:25: "These things have I spoken unto you... [to verse 31]... let us go hence." 
Eucharistic Hymn [Serbasatsoutiun]: "Angelic" (Hreshtakayin). 
But when the celebrant reaches that place where he says: "Take, eat," the clerks shall sing the hymn, Tone 2 [by]: "Awesome wind" (Sarsapeli hoghmoy). 
Prayer with kneeling: "Let us pray to the Lord" (Ztēr aghachesgouk), three times. 
Then: "And again in peace." 
"Blessing and glory." 
And then they shall read the three Prayers of Saint John Chrysostom, which were spoken to the Holy Trinity: "Almighty, undefiled God." 
And at the completion: "Heavenly Father" (Hayr yerk). 
Hymn, Tone 2 [bk]: "Today the heavenly hosts" (Aysor yerknayinkn). 
Prayer with kneeling

## page_0503_human

CANON
OF THE ASCENSION OF CHRIST OUR GOD
__________________
THURSDAY. THE FEAST OF THE ASCENSION
of Christ into heaven, which is the
fortieth day from Easter. In the
Night [Service], Processional hymn [Hetevak sharakan], Tone 8 [dk], "Greatly Wondrous" (Metsahrash).
And the priests shall vest together and come
into the chancel, and begin the canticle of the dawn [ah.], Tone 5 [g3],
"He Ascended" (Hambartsaw). Father [hymn], Tone 8 [dk], "Blessed is the crown
of sweetness." Magnificat [Mets.], "Mother of Light, Mother of God."
Song [of the Resurrection], "He ascended,
God, with blessing." Litany, "Let us say."
"Holy God, who didst ascend in glory to the Father." (Until the Coming of the
Spirit, the Song, Litany, and the "Holy God"
shall be said thus).
And they shall go forth with the cross and the Gospel in procession, singing the
hymn [sharakan], Tone 7 [gdz], "He Ascended."
Psalm 46 [47], "God ascended with blessing."
Scripture, Song of Songs 3:6, "Who is this that goeth up..." to chapter 4:3, "...from thy silence."
Prophecy, Isaiah 63:1, "And who is this..." to verse 6, "...them to the earth."
Acts 1:1, "The first book..." to verse 14, "...His brothers."
Alleluia, Psalm 46 [47], "God ascended."
Gospel, Luke 24:41, "And while they yet..." to verse 53, "...and blessing God."
Hymn [sharakan], Tone 7 [gdz], "Our Lord Christ Ascended."
After the "Stand up" (Ort'i), Gospel,
Mark 16:9, "Jesus having risen..." to verse 20, "...following them."
And they shall bless the four corners of the world [Andastan]. Litany, "By this Holy Cross."
Prayer, "Protect [us]." And then:
Psalm 23 [24], chanted: "Lift up [your gates]." Gospel,
Luke 18:1, "He spoke also a parable..." to
verse 14, "...shall be exalted." Litany, "Let us ask,"
"Save [us]." Prayer, "By Thy almighty."
At the Midday Liturgy, Introit [Zhamamut], "The heavenly powers were amazed,"
and "Who ineffably." Antiphon [P'okh], "Praise Jerusalem."
Hymn [sharakan], Tone 1 [adz], "He who ascended."
Psalm 118 [119], "Blessed are they..." [namely the sections:] "My portion...", "I have done judgment...", "In my distress...", "I lifted up [my eyes]."
Acts 1:1, "The first book..." to verse 14,
"...His brothers." Alleluia, Psalm 23 [24], "Lift up,
O princes, [your gates]." Gospel, Matthew
28:16, "Now the eleven disciples..." to verse
20, "...unto the end of the world." Hagiography [Srbasatsut'iun],
"Who is like [unto Thee]."

In the Evening
they shall go forth with the cross and the Gospel
and all decorations in procession, singing
the hymn [sharakan], Tone 7 [gdz], "Our Lord
Christ Ascended." Psalm, "God ascended
with blessing." They shall read the morning
Scriptures and the Gospels, and
they shall bless the four corners of the world [Andastan]. Litany,
"By this Holy Cross." Prayer, "Protect [us]." And
then, chanted, Alleluia, Psalm 46 [47], "God
ascended with blessing." Gospel,
Matthew 19:1, "And it came to pass when He finished..."
to verse 15, "...He departed thence." And then, "Bow down [thy ear]" and "Live."
Messiah [Mese], "The God of gods," "From the rising of the sun."
Hambartsi [Hmb.], Tone 5 [g3],
"Who are co-planted," and "Our Lord ascended
into heaven." Dismissal Psalm 23 [24], "Lift up..."

From here until the third Sunday of Vardavar:
Everything set down remains always unchanged; only the Resurrection Hymns of the Fathers (Harts) change at times, which [you will find] in the Directory.

And the rest is of the Resurrection.

Begin the Resurrection Hymns of Ascent (Hambartsis) from here: say them on Sundays according to the proper tone. And on whichever Sunday another feast should occur, when you do not take the Resurrection Hymn of Ascent, keep it in mind; when a Sunday occurs in that same tone, say both Hymns of Ascent, as the Directory shows you.

Litany: "By the holy ascetics."
Prayer: "Crown-giver."
And then say "Arevelits" (Of the East) without fail. "Save us" (Ketso) is not used except on Sundays and Dominical Feasts.
Do likewise for all feasts of the martyrs.

Supplication: The Most High. Litany: And we in unison. Prayer: Hope of life. Our Father. And then Peace Service: without fail.

Tuesday. Tone 3 [b3]: Of the Holy Virgins Gaiane and her companions. In the Evening, perform the Pre-festive service [Nakhatonak]: Souls [Anzink] with its proper hymns. Likewise also in the night, sing the canon-blessing [Ah.], the Patrum [Harsh], the Magnificat [Mkk.], and the Hambartsi [Hmb.] of Saint Hripsime and her companions. Morning song: They shall boast. Midday Liturgy Office Hour [Introit]: The victorious. Hymn Tone 7 [dz]: Christ God. Psalm 44 [45]: She shall be brought. Proverbs 1:20: Wisdom, to verse 23: of reproof. Prophet Jeremiah (Baruch) 4:36: Look, to 5:4: of godliness. Apostle 2 Corinthians 6:16: For you are, to 7:1: with fear of God. Alleluia: The queen shall stand. Gospel John 16:1: These things I have spoken, to verse 5: because I was with you.

Keep the order of the Patrum [Harsh] of Repentance in this manner.
Starting from this Wednesday, on all Wednesdays and fast days of the week on which there are no feasts,
say in order all the Patrum [Harsh] of Repentance except for two, Tone 2 [bk] and Tone 8 [dk] composition; that is, except for the Patrum [Harsh] "God powerful" and "Your name forever."
And upon finishing them, begin again to say only the fast [alts] hymns until you reach the Sunday of Saint Sarkis; the Directory also shows you this. And
as for the canon-blessings [Orh.] of such fasts and of Fridays, say the Repentance Miserere [Oghormya] in the tone of the day.
Likewise also in the evenings: say the Messedi and the Hambartsi of Repentance in the tone of the day.
Wednesday. Tone 4 [bk]. Patrum [Harsh]: God, Who lookest with sweetness. Morning song: I...

Canon of Saint Gregory our Enlightener, on his coming out of the pit.
Saturday: Feast of Saint Gregory our Enlightener, on his coming out of the pit. In the evening, perform a pre-festive service [Nakhatonak]: "O blessed one" with its proper canons. [Mankunk], [Tone] 3 [Gy]: "Wonderful God" and "Who were called".
Midday Introit: "Rejoice today". Hymn, [Tone] Plagal 3 [Gk]: "Good Shepherd". Psalm 39 [40]: "He drew me up from the pit". Proverbs / Wisdom of Solomon 5:1 "Then the righteous man" to verse 8 "profitable for us".

Dismissal hymn [Hambartsi], [Tone] 3 [Gy]: "Rejoice, O Holy Church". After this, Litany: "Let us ask", "Save". Prayer: "King of peace".

Second Sunday after the Descent of the Holy Spirit: [Tone] Plagal 4 [Dk]. Canticle of the Fathers [Harts], [Tone] 3 [Gy]: "Today Bright". Magnificat, [Tone] 3 [Gy]: "Of the Unoriginated Word". Song: "To Your House", Verse: "The Lord reigns". Litany: "Let us say". "Holy God, who wast crucified." And they shall go forth with the Cross and the Gospel in procession, singing the hymn, [Tone] Plagal 4 [Dk]: "The Only-Begotten descended from the Father". Psalm 117 [118]: "This is the day", Verse: "Give thanks".
Proverbs 9:1 "Wisdom has built" to verse 6 "understanding with knowledge."

And they shall sign with the Cross the four corners of the world [Andastan]. Litany: "By Your Holy Cross". Prayer: "Keep us". And then they shall begin the Hymn of Rest, [Tone] Plagal 4 [Dk]: "King of Glory", "Holy Virgin Mother of God", "Who art unoriginated from the Existent", Glory to the Father. Psalm 129 [130]: "Out of the depths I cried". Gospel according to Matthew 13:31 "Another parable" to verse 35 "from the foundation of the world." For the Souls. "For the departed". "O Christ, Son of God".
After this: "Newly-created". Litany: "Let us ask", "Save". Prayer: "To Your All-powerful".
Midday Introit: "Peace [to all]". Hymn, [Tone] 3 [Gy]: "Today rejoicing". Psalm, Scripture, and Gospel are those of the morning. Eucharistic Hymn: "Angelic".
In the evening, at the tenth hour, they shall go forth with the Cross and the Gospel and with all adornments in procession, singing the hymn, [Tone] Plagal 4 [Dk]: "The Only-Begotten descended from the Father". Psalm: "This is the day which the Lord has made", Verse: "Give thanks". They shall read the morning scriptures. After this, Alleluia: "There was signed". Gospel according to Matthew 24:27 "For as the lightning" to verse 36 "my words shall not pass away." And they shall sign with the Cross the four corners of the world [Andastan]. Litany: "By Your Holy Cross". Prayer: "Keep us". And then: "Incline Your ear" and "Deliver us". Mesedi: "Now bless [the Lord]", "You who stand in the house". Litany: "Let us say". On the feast of Martyrs and other common saints who do not have a proper blessing: from the Tone of the day, say the Martyrs' "The Lord in heaven" [Ter yerknits], and then one more Hymn of other saints according to the propriety of the day; and after the Coming of the Spirit, and the rest according to order.

Monday. Tone 3 [gd]: Of the holy Infants of Bethlehem, and of Akakios the witness, and of Movkima the priest, and of Kodratos the soldier. Canticle [Harts] Tone 3 [dg]: "Today the innocent holy" with its proper parts. Midday Liturgy Psalm 8: "Out of the mouths of babes". Proverbs 29:2 "In praise of" to verse 7 "righteous minds". Prophet Isaiah 18:7 "In that time" to verse 7 "Mount Zion". Apostle Hebrews 2:14 "Forasmuch then" to verse 18 "to succor". Alleluia: "The Lord loveth justice". Gospel Matthew 2:16 "Then when he saw" to verse 18 "because they were not".

Tuesday. Tone 8 [gk]: Of the holy virgins Nune and Mane. Say the Blessing, the Canticle [Harts], and all the rest of Saint Hripsime. Midday Liturgy Introit: "Betrothed of Christ". Psalm 44 [45]: "They shall be brought". Book of Song of Songs 8:14 "Flee, my beloved" to verse 24 "spotless". Prophet Isaiah 27:11 "Women who come" to verse 13 "in Jerusalem". Catholic Epistle 1 Peter 5:8 "Be sober" to verse 11 "forever, amen". Alleluia: "She shall stand". Gospel John 12:24 "Verily, verily" to verse 26 "him my Father".

Wednesday. Tone 2 [bd]: Canticle [Harts] Tone 3 [gd]: "Who co-eternal". Midday Liturgy Psalm 65 [66]: "Save Thy servants". Apostle Romans 4:13 "For not if" to verse 22 "unto righteousness". Alleluia: "My heart was troubled". Gospel Matthew 10:34 "Think not" to verse 42 "his reward".

And regarding the Martyrs' Canticles [Harts], know this:
From this Thursday, make a beginning from the first Tone to the last Tone, and say them in order; and when they end, begin again. But Tone 3 [gd] "Who boundless", Tone 8 [gk] "The military hosts", and Tone 4 [dd] "Who participated" — do not say these three Canticles in order, because they have their own proper days.

Thursday. Tone 4 [bk]: Of the holy princes Sahak and Joseph, and the witnesses Sarkis and Bakhos. Blessing [of the Tone] of the Martyrs. Canticle [Harts] Tone 1 [ad]: "Thee the righteous". Midday Liturgy Psalm 115 [116]: "Precious is", Response: "I believed". Proverbs 6:1 "My son, if thou be" to verse 5 "from the snares". Prophet Isaiah 33:5 "Holy is the Lord" to verse 8 "them among men". Apostle Romans 8:18 "For I reckon" to verse 27 "for the saints". Alleluia: "The righteous cried". Gospel Luke 21:12 "But before" to verse 19 "your souls".

Friday. Tone 1 [ad]: Canticle [Harts] of Rest Tone 3 [gd]: "Who art the King". Midday Liturgy Psalm 97 [98]: "Have seen all". Apostle Romans 5:12 "For as by one" to verse 21 "our Lord". Alleluia: "For Thy name's sake". Gospel Matthew 11:25 "At that time" to verse 30 "faint-hearted".

Saturday. Tone 8 [gk]: Of the holy ones Nerses the Great, our patriarch, and Khat the bishop. Blessing Tone 4 [bk]: "Who the unutterable light". Canticle [Harts] Tone 3 [gd]: "Today the church celebrates" with its proper parts. Midday Liturgy Psalm 131 [132]: "Priests". Wisdom of Solomon 2:23 "For God" to 3:8 "forever". Prophet Isaiah 57:15 "Thus saith the high" to verse 16 "I made". Apostle Hebrews 13:7 "Remember your leaders" to verse 9 "so walked". Alleluia: "Thy priests". Gospel Matthew 10:16 "Behold, I" to verse 22 "he shall stand". In the Evening: "Joyful Light", and the rest according to order.

Fourth Sunday after the Coming of the Spirit. Tone 4 [dd]: Canticle [Harts] Tone 1 [ad]: "Be Thou brightened". Magnificat [Metsatsustse] Tone 1 [ad]: "Who wast chosen from the beginning". Midday Liturgy Psalm 64 [65]: "To Thee is due". Prophet Isaiah 1:1 "Hear, O heaven" to verse 15 "are full of blood". Psalm: "Holy is Thy temple". Apostle Romans 6:12 "Let not therefore" to verse 23 "in our Lord". Alleluia: "Thou hast been pleased, Lord". Gospel Matthew 12:1 "At that time went" to verse 8 "Son of man". In the Evening, Messedi: "Let Thy hand be", "Who shepherdest". Dismissal Hymn [Hambartsi] Tone 4 [dd]: "Glorifier". Proclamation: "Let us ask", "Save us". Prayer: "King of peace". And then the Fast of Saint Gregory our Illuminator; but there are feasts.

Epistle Romans 11:1 "Now I say then" to verse 12 "their fullness." Alleluia: "Lord, rebuke me not in your anger." Gospel Matthew 13:43 "Then shall the righteous" to verse 52 "things new and old."

Saturday. Tone 3 [b-3]. Feast of the Holy Twelve Apostles of Christ, and of Saint Paul the thirteenth Apostle. Night Office [ah.] Tone 6 [g-3] "Blessed," with its canon. Morning Canticle [Mkk.] Tone 8 [d-k] "Apostles of God." Midday Introit: "Apostles." Hymn [Sharakan] Tone 2 [a-k] "In the infinite." Psalm 18:4 [19:4] "Through all the earth." (Note: This is not in the Bible, see the Lectionary [Chashots]). Epistle 1 Thessalonians 2:9 "Remember, brethren" to verse 16 "to the uttermost." Alleluia: "You shall make them." Gospel Matthew 9:36 "And seeing" to 10:7 "the kingdom of heaven." In the Evening: Evening Litany [Hambartsi] Tone 1 [a-dz] "Holy are you, O Lord" and "From you we entreat."

Sixth Sunday after Pentecost. Tone 4 [b-k]. Eve of the Fast of the Transfiguration [Vardavar], always. Canticle of the Council [Harts] Tone 2 [a-k] "Who for our salvation." Magnificat [Mets.] Tone 2 [a-k] "Of your new tabernacle." Midday Liturgy: Psalm 64 [65] "Praise is due to you." Prophet Isaiah 3:1 "Behold, the Lord" to verse 11 "shall pass through it." Epistle Romans 11:13 "But I speak to you" to verse 24 "into the olive tree." Alleluia: "Arise, Lord, in your command." Gospel Matthew 14:13 "And when he heard" to verse 21 "and children." In the Evening: Scripture [Mesedi] "Sing," "Enter." Evening Litany [Hamb.] Tone 4 [b-k] "Of your resurrection." And then the Fast of the Transfiguration [Vardavar] begins, but no feasts are held.

Monday. Tone 3 [b-3]. Canticle of the Council [Harts] Tone 1 [a-3] "Blessed are you forever." Midday Liturgy: Psalm 144 [145] "Your kingdom." Epistle Romans 11:25 "I would not, brethren" to verse 36 "glory forever, Amen." Alleluia: "Come, let us rejoice." Gospel Matthew 14:22 "And he constrained" to verse 36 "they were saved."

CANON
OF THE GLORIOUS TRANSFIGURATION OF CHRIST OUR GOD
ON MOUNT TABOR
WHICH IS VARDAVAR SUNDAY.

In the Night [Service] there is a vigil. They shall place the Holy Sacrament upon the cushion-throne in the chancel. Processional Hymn, Tone 8 [dzk]: "This day in glory rejoices" (Tsndzay aysor parok). And the priests shall vest together and come out to the chancel, having lighted candles in their hands, and they shall begin the Blessing Hymn, Tone 1 [adz]: "He who was transfigured on the mountain" (Vor i lerinn aylakerpyal). Alleluia. "Yours are the heavens." Verse: "The north and the south." Alternate [verse]: "The heights of the mountains." "Come, let us worship." Canticle of the Fathers, Tone 2 [ak]: "The ineffable" (Zanchareli), with its proper canon. And they shall go out in procession with the Cross and the Gospel and all adornments, and they shall say the "Lord of Heaven" [hymns]. Litany: "Let us say." [Trisagion]: "Holy God, who wast revealed on Mount Tabor." And then Hymn, Tone 1 [adz]: "He who was transfigured on the mountain." Afterwards, Psalm 97 [98]: "The mountains shall rejoice." Verse: "Bless." Proverbs of Solomon [Ecclesiastes] 11:9: "Rejoice, O young man" to chapter 12:7: "who gave it." Prophet Isaiah 34:20: "Behold Zion" to verse 22: "He will save us." Catholic [Epistle], 2 Peter 1:12: "Wherefore I intend" to verse 19: "in your hearts." Alleluia: "The heights of the mountains." Gospel according to Mark 8:27: "And Jesus went out" to chapter 9:12: "of him." And they shall bless the four corners of the world [Andastan]. Litany: "By this Holy Cross." Prayer: "Guardian." And upon returning, Hymn, Tone 8 [dk], melody: "The mountains shall rejoice." And then "Newly-created" (Norasteghtsyal). Litany: "Let us ask," "Save us." Prayer: "To your almighty and wondrous Transfiguration."

At the Midday Liturgy, Introit: "Your Lordship is revealed today." Verse: "My soul shall magnify." Hymn, Tone 3 [bz]: "Rejoice, O crowned." Psalm 14 [15]: "Lord, who shall abide." Proverbs of Wisdom 7:25: "For she is the breath" to chapter 8:4: "of his works." Prophet Zechariah 14:16: "And it shall come to pass, that everyone" to verse 21: "in that day." Catholic [Epistle], 1 John 1:1: "That which was from the beginning" to verse 7: "from all sin." Alleluia: "A bright cloud." Gospel according to Matthew 16:13: "When Jesus came" to chapter 17:13: "he spoke unto them." Eucharistic Hymn [Srbatsutyun]: "Multitudes."

In the Evening at the tenth hour they shall assemble in the church, and they shall begin in order the morning psalm and read the scriptures, as written above. And at the conclusion, Orthi [Stand up]. Gospel according to Matthew 17:14: "And when they were come" to verse 20: "and fasting." And then they shall go out in procession with the Cross and the Gospel. Hymn, Tone 5 [gdz]: "On Mount Tabor" (I tabor i lerinn). Afterwards, Orthi [Stand up]. Gospel according to Mark 8:39 [9:1]: "And he said unto them" to chapter 9:12: "of him." Hymn, Tone 8 [dzk]: "Today the mystery" (Aysor zkhorhurt). Afterwards, Orthi [Stand up]. Gospel according to John 7:1: "After these things Jesus walked" to verse 13: "for fear of the Jews." And they shall bless the four corners of the world [Andastan]. Litany: "By this Holy Cross."

From this point until the end, [the services] are for the most part movable; for this reason, the Patristic Canticles, tones, and other variables are not set down hereafter, which you must look up in the Directory. But the feast of Saint Thaddeus the Apostle and the coming two weeks—although they are placed here as immovable—nevertheless sometimes shift, as you see from letter 'Ch' [Չ] of the Directory until 'K' [Ք]. For this reason you must always have the Directory at hand and be guided by it until the end.

If, according to the calendar, a fourth Sunday is required here, see the second Sunday after the Octave. If a fifth is also required, see the third Sunday after the Octave. But if a sixth is also required, then place the first Sunday after the Octave here in place of the fourth; place the second Sunday here in place of the fifth; and place the third Sunday here in place of the sixth. Processional [Mankunk] and Hymn at the Dismissal [Hambartsi], Tone 3 [gk]: Superior among the prophets. Midday Psalm 98 [99]: God is glorified; response [Pokh]: Of mercy. Proverbs 20:6: Great is man, to verse 22: he shall be a helper. Book of Job 42:12: And the Lord blessed, to verse 16: from Abraham. Prophet Jeremiah, Lamentations 3:22: The mercies of the Lord, to verse 56: to my voice (selected reading). Acts 13:25: And as John fulfilled, to verse 33: having raised up Jesus. Catholic Epistle: James 5:7: Be patient, to verse 11: and merciful. Alleluia: And you, child. Gospel: Matthew 11:1: At that time, to verse 12: they told Jesus.

Friday
Canticle of the Fathers for Rest. Midday Psalm 97 [98]: All have seen. Epistle: 2 Corinthians 5:6: Therefore we are always confident, to verse 18: of reconciliation. Alleluia: according to the Tone. Gospel: Mark 7:24: And from thence he arose, to verse 30: on the bed.

Saturday
Of the Holy Council of Nicaea: the three hundred and eighteen patriarchs. Hymn of the Sunrise [Aravotyan Orhnutiun], Tone 4 [dk]: Today the holy patriarchs, with its proper canon. Processional [Mankunk], Tone 3 [gts]: O wondrous; followed by: In the Holy Trinity. Midday Entrance [Zhamamut]: The patriarchal. Hymn, Tone 3 [gts]: Today the heavenly ones. Psalm: Thy priests. Prophet Ezekiel 44:23: Thus says the Lord to the priests, to verse 24: they shall keep my feasts. Epistle: Hebrews 12:1: Wherefore seeing we also, to verse 7: God dealeth with you. Alleluia: Thy priests. Gospel: John 14:25: These things have I spoken unto you, to verse 31: let us go hence.

Understand, O lover of feasts, if the interval of eating meat is three weeks, these are the feasts that are set down up to this point. And the fourth Sunday becomes the Eve of the Fast of the Holy Cross; at that time, the feasts set down below are celebrated in another place. But if the meat-eating period is four weeks, add one week here, in which the feasts set down below are celebrated, and the fifth Sunday becomes the Eve of the Fast. And in such years, sometimes the Apostles James and Simon, being separated from Thomas, are celebrated on the Saturday of this week. The Directory [Tarekirk] shows you all this.

Fourth Sunday after the Holy Mother of God. Song of the Resurrection. Midday Psalm 64 [65]: Praise is due to Thee.
